Compose only wires env/networks/volumes/labels. After editing a baked-in config file: `docker compose up -d --build <service>`.\n- Networks: `proxy` (external-facing, shared with Harbor by name), `data` (internal; pgbouncer/minio/redis), `db` (internal; postgres↔pgbouncer only), `nohrpls` (non-internal — background worker needs outbound internet; pins DNS to 1.1.1.1/8.8.8.8), `socket` (internal; traefik↔socket-proxy only).\n- **Traefik has no docker.sock mount** — reads the Docker API via `socket-proxy` (GET-only, CONTAINERS+NETWORKS+INFO; writes get 403). CrowdSec reads traefik's logs through it too.\n- **Every websecure request passes a middleware chain** (real-IP → crowdsec → secure-headers) — full detail in [docs/crowdsec.md](docs/crowdsec.md).\n- All state is bind-mounted inside this tree (`*/data` dirs) so restic backs up one directory.\n\nAdding a web service: folder with Dockerfile → service in `compose.yml` on `proxy` (+ `data` if it needs pg/redis/minio) → traefik labels (Host rule, entrypoints websecure, certresolver `${CERT_RESOLVER:-letsencrypt}`, loadbalancer port) → `docker compose up -d --build`. No DNS/cert step (wildcard record + wildcard cert cover new subdomains) unless it needs big uploads → add a DNS-only (grey cloud) record instead.\n\n```bash\ncd /home/scriptkid/opt/cloud\ndocker compose config -q            # validate after edits\ndocker compose up -d --build        # apply changes\ndocker ps                           # everything should be healthy\n```\n\n## TLS / ACME\n\nTwo resolvers in `traefik/traefik.yml`:\n- `cloudflare` — DNS-01, **the current default** (`CERT_RESOLVER=cloudflare` in both `.env` and `harbor/2.15.2/.env`), serves a wildcard `*.uchabokeria.space` cert. Needs `CF_DNS_API_TOKEN` (Zone/DNS/Edit) in `.env`.\n- `letsencrypt` — HTTP-01 fallback, needs nothing.\n\nA **wildcard DNS record** (`*.uchabokeria.space`, proxied) exists → new subdomains need no DNS/cert step. Cert storage: `traefik/acme/*.json` (must stay chmod 600).\n\n**Cloudflare caveats:**\n- SSL/TLS mode must be **Full (strict)** — \"Flexible\" causes infinite redirect loops with Traefik's 80→443 redirect.\n- **registry.uchabokeria.space is DNS-only (grey cloud) since 2026-07-03** — CF free plan caps request bodies at 100MB, breaking `docker push` of larger layers. Don't re-enable the proxy on it. Same applies to `s3.` if big uploads fail.\n\n## Reboot recovery\n\nThis host reboots often. `boot.sh` runs from crontab `@reboot`: waits for the docker daemon, then `docker compose up -d`'s every project in its `PROJECTS` array (cloud stack + Harbor). Add new compose projects to that array. If a domain 404s after reboot, read `boot.log` and `docker ps -a` first. (Harbor doesn't self-recover after reboots — see [docs/harbor.md](docs/harbor.md).)\n\n**The Claude/Telegram session self-heals too**: `telegram/poller-watchdog.sh` runs from crontab every 5 min — restarts the session if tmux or the `bun server.ts` poller is gone (3-miss delay + 30-min cooldown, because a restart wipes session context), and Telegram-alerts on an expired login, which no restart can fix. Log: `telegram/watchdog.log`. Added after the bot sat dead-but-alive for a month (2026-07-18 → 08-19); see [docs/telegram-widget.md](docs/telegram-widget.md).\n\nA second watchdog, `telegram/limit-watchdog.sh` (crontab every 2 min, same log), covers **usage-limit stalls**. Claude Code's native auto-resume handles the ordinary 5-hour case, but gives up in three states that leave a detached tmux session parked forever: `stale` (\"press enter to continue\" — nothing presses Enter on a server), `cap-exhausted` (disarmed after repeated hits), and `horizon-exceeded` (weekly limit, >24 h out). The watchdog sends the Enter, re-sends Claude's own continue prompt, or parks until the weekly reset, and Telegram-alerts each time. It acts **only** on those exact pane strings — never on \"session looks idle\", which would invent work. It scans every open Claude tmux session (`claude` plus `cl-*` from `/new`), and reads reset epochs from `~/.claude/rate-limit-state/*.json`, written by the statusline. Plan and the string-by-string reasoning: [docs/session-autocontinue-plan.md](docs/session-autocontinue-plan.md).\n\n## Secrets\n\n- Root `.env` (chmod 600): all stack credentials incl. Intended for trusted networks or VPN connections rather than direct public internet exposure.\n\n```bash\n# 1. Set the dashboard login credentials in ~/.hermes/.env (secrets file, 0600).\ncat >> ~/.hermes/.env <<'EOF'\nHERMES_DASHBOARD_BASIC_AUTH_USERNAME=admin\nHERMES_DASHBOARD_BASIC_AUTH_PASSWORD=choose-a-strong-password\n# Recommended: a stable signing secret so sessions survive restarts.\nHERMES_DASHBOARD_BASIC_AUTH_SECRET=$(openssl rand -base64 32)\nEOF\nchmod 600 ~/.hermes/.env\n\n# 2. Run the dashboard bound to a reachable address. Allows X-Forwarded-Proto headers to be trusted securely without accepting unbounded entries like 0.0.0.0/0.\n\n```yaml\ndashboard:\n  public_url: \"https://dashboard.example.com\"\n  trusted_proxies:\n    - \"172.20.0.5\"\n    # Or, if the proxy address is dynamic on a dedicated network:\n    # - \"172.20.0.0/24\"\n```\n\n### Public URL override\n\nSource: https://github.com/nousresearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/website/docs/user-guide/features/web-dashboard.md\n\nBy default, the dashboard reconstructs the OAuth callback URL from the request — `X-Forwarded-Host` + `X-Forwarded-Proto` + `X-Forwarded-Prefix` (when uvicorn is configured with `proxy_headers=True`, which `start_server` enables under the gate). This works out of the box behind a reverse proxy that sets all three headers correctly.\n\nFor deploys behind reverse proxies that don't reliably forward those headers (manual nginx setups, on-prem ingresses, custom-domain deploys with partial proxy chains), set `dashboard.public_url` (or `HERMES_DASHBOARD_PUBLIC_URL`) to the **complete public URL** the dashboard is reached at:\n\nWhen set, the OAuth callback URL becomes `<public_url>/auth/callback` verbatim — `X-Forwarded-Prefix` is ignored on that code path because the operator has explicitly declared the public URL. This is intentional: stacking the prefix on top would double-prefix the common case where the prefix is already baked into `public_url`.\n\nThe hostname in `public_url` is also accepted as an **exact** HTTP `Host` and\nWebSocket `Origin` value. This supports a reverse proxy that preserves the\nbrowser-facing hostname while forwarding to a dashboard bound to\n`127.0.0.1`. Wildcards and suffix matches are not allowed, so an attacker host\nsuch as `dashboard.example.com.evil.test` remains rejected by the DNS-rebinding\nguard."}]}

Add multiple connections and switch between them with one tap — for example, a home server and a work server, or a dev instance and a production one.\n\n## What a connection contains\n\nEach connection has a stable ID for one Hermes install and can hold several\nindependent capabilities:\n\n- Dashboard/Gateway URL (`http(s)://host:9119`) and dashboard session for primary Chat, sessions, Manage, and standard voice\n- Optional API server URL (`http(s)://host:8642`) and API key for explicit Direct API or advanced headless compatibility\n- Optional Relay URL (`ws(s)://host:8767`) and pairing record for Terminal, Bridge, and relay-only power tools\n- Connection-scoped Dashboard cookies or native sign-in tokens, API credentials,\n  and Relay session credentials; authentication never carries into another\n  saved connection\n- Its own sessions, memory, personalities, and skill list (fetched from that server)\n- Last-active session ID and explicit profile pick, so switching back takes you where you left off\n\nConnections do **not** share sessions, memory, or personalities. Each Hermes server is a separate world. What *is* shared across connections: the app theme and settings, bridge safety preferences (blocklist, destructive-verb confirmation, auto-disable), and the device's TOFU cert-pin record for each host.\n\n## Switching connection\n\nTap the agent's name or avatar in the chat top bar to open the agent info sheet. Its **Connection** section lists your connections as a radio list — each row shows the connection's label plus its hostname and pairing status. Tap another to switch. The list appears only when you have two or more connections (with a single connection there's nothing to switch between), and it's disabled while a reply is streaming.\n\nOn switch, the app:\n\n1. Cancels any in-flight chat stream.\n2. Disconnects from the current relay.\n3. Rebinds to the selected connection's endpoints.\n4. Reconnects and reloads sessions, personalities, and skills from the new server.\n5. Restores the last-active session and profile pick on that connection, if there was one.\n\nThe whole thing takes under a second on a healthy connection.\n\n## Managing connections\n\nOpen **Settings → Connections** for the connection **list**. Each card shows the\nconnection's label, an **Active** badge on the one in use, a one-line status, and\na compact capability summary (Gateway · Manage · Voice · Direct API · Relay) so you can scan the\nhealth of every server at a glance. Write minimal code to pass.\n\n**Core principle:** If you didn't watch the test fail, you don't know if it tests the right thing.\n\n**Violating the letter of the rules is violating the spirit of the rules.**\n\n## When to Use\n\n**Always:**\n- New features\n- Bug fixes\n- Refactoring\n- Behavior changes\n\n**Exceptions (ask your human partner):**\n- Throwaway prototypes\n- Generated code\n- Configuration files\n\nThinking \"skip TDD just this once\"? The reviewer gets precisely crafted context for evaluation — never your session's history.\n\n**Core principle:** Review early, review often.\n\n## When to Request Review\n\n**Mandatory:**\n- After each task in subagent-driven development\n- After completing major feature\n- Before merge to main\n\n**Optional but valuable:**\n- When stuck (fresh perspective)\n- Before refactoring (baseline check)\n- After fixing complex bug\n\n## How to Request\n\n**1. Get git SHAs:**\n```bash\nBASE_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD~1)  # or: git merge-base origin/main HEAD\nHEAD_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n```\n\n**2. Dispatch code reviewer subagent:**\n\nDispatch a `general-purpose` subagent, filling the template at [code-reviewer.md](code-reviewer.md)\n\n**Placeholders:**\n- `{DESCRIPTION}` - Brief summary of what you built\n- `{PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS}` - What it should do\n- `{BASE_SHA}` - Starting commit\n- `{HEAD_SHA}` - Ending commit\n\n**3. Act on feedback:**\n- Fix Critical issues immediately\n- Fix Important issues before proceeding\n- Note Minor issues for later\n- Push back if reviewer is wrong (with reasoning)\n\n## Example\n\n```\n[Just completed Task 2: Add verification function]\n\nYou: Let me request code review before proceeding.\n\nBASE_SHA=$(git log --oneline | grep \"Task 1\" | head -1 | awk '{print $1}')\nHEAD_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n\n[Dispatch code reviewer subagent]\n  DESCRIPTION: Added verifyIndex() and repairIndex() with 4 issue types\n  PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS: Task 2 from docs/superpowers/plans/deployment-plan.md\n  BASE_SHA: a7981ec\n  HEAD_SHA: 3df7661\n\n[Subagent returns]:\n  Strengths: Clean architecture, real tests\n  Issues:\n    Important: Missing progress indicators\n    Minor: Magic number (100) for reporting interval\n  Assessment: Ready to proceed\n\nYou: [Fix progress indicators]\n[Continue to Task 3]\n```\n\n## Common Rationalizations\n\n| Excuse | Reality |\n|--------|---------|\n| \"I'll just review the diff myself instead of dispatching a reviewer\" | You're the coordinator — reviewing the diff inline burns the context window you need to keep driving the work. Dispatch a reviewer subagent: the diff and the evaluation live in its context, and only the findings come back to you. |\n| \"The reviewer needs my whole session history to understand the change\" | Hand it precisely crafted context, never your session's history. That keeps the reviewer on the work product, not your thought process. |\n\n## Red Flags\n\n**Never:**\n- Skip review because \"it's simple\"\n- Ignore Critical issues\n- Proceed with unfixed Important issues\n- Argue with valid technical feedback\n\n**If reviewer wrong:**\n- Push back with technical reasoning\n- Show code/tests that prove it works\n- Request clarification\n\nSee template at: [code-reviewer.md](code-reviewer.md)\n"}

They are design content, not decoration. Bring the same intentionality and minimalism to copywriting that you would bring to spacing and color. Before writing anything, ask what the design needs to say, and how it can best be said to help the person navigate the experience.\n\nWrite from the end user's perspective. Name things by what users will understand in simple language, not by how the system is built. A user manages notifications, not webhook config. Describe what something is or does in plain terms rather than selling it. Being specific and legible to new users is always better than being clever.\n\nUse active voice as default. A CTA says exactly what happens when it is used: \"Save changes,\" not \"Submit.\" An action keeps the same name through the whole flow, so the button that says \"Publish\" produces a toast that says \"Published.\" The vocabulary of an interface is the signposting for someone navigating the product. Cohesion and consistency are how people learn their way around.\n\nTreat failure and emptiness as moments for direction, not mood. Explain what went wrong and how to fix it, in the interface's voice rather than a person's. Errors don't apologize, and they are never vague about what happened. An empty screen is an invitation to act.\n\nKeep the tone conversational: plain verbs, sentence case, no filler, with tone matched to the brand and the audience. Add multiple connections and switch between them with one tap — for example, a home server and a work server, or a dev instance and a production one.\n\n## What a connection contains\n\nEach connection has a stable ID for one Hermes install and can hold several\nindependent capabilities:\n\n- Dashboard/Gateway URL (`http(s)://host:9119`) and dashboard session for primary Chat, sessions, Manage, and standard voice\n- Optional API server URL (`http(s)://host:8642`) and API key for explicit Direct API or advanced headless compatibility\n- Optional Relay URL (`ws(s)://host:8767`) and pairing record for Terminal, Bridge, and relay-only power tools\n- Connection-scoped Dashboard cookies or native sign-in tokens, API credentials,\n  and Relay session credentials; authentication never carries into another\n  saved connection\n- Its own sessions, memory, personalities, and skill list (fetched from that server)\n- Last-active session ID and explicit profile pick, so switching back takes you where you left off\n\nConnections do **not** share sessions, memory, or personalities. Each Hermes server is a separate world. What *is* shared across connections: the app theme and settings, bridge safety preferences (blocklist, destructive-verb confirmation, auto-disable), and the device's TOFU cert-pin record for each host.\n\n## Switching connection\n\nTap the agent's name or avatar in the chat top bar to open the agent info sheet. Its **Connection** section lists your connections as a radio list — each row shows the connection's label plus its hostname and pairing status. Tap another to switch. The list appears only when you have two or more connections (with a single connection there's nothing to switch between), and it's disabled while a reply is streaming.\n\nOn switch, the app:\n\n1. Cancels any in-flight chat stream.\n2. Disconnects from the current relay.\n3. Rebinds to the selected connection's endpoints.\n4. Reconnects and reloads sessions, personalities, and skills from the new server.\n"}

---- session lifecycle -------------------------------------------------\n\n    def verify_session(self, *, access_token: str) -> Optional[Session]:\n        payload = _unsign(access_token, self._secret, \"access\")\n        if payload is None:\n            return None\n        return self._session(str(payload.get(\"sub\", \"\")), int(payload[\"exp\"]), access_token, \"\")\n\n    def refresh_session(self, *, refresh_token: str) -> Session:\n        if not refresh_token:\n            raise RefreshExpiredError(\"no refresh token present in session\")\n        payload = _unsign(refresh_token, self._secret, \"refresh\")\n        if payload is None:\n            raise RefreshExpiredError(\"refresh token expired or invalid\")\n        return self._mint_session(str(payload.get(\"sub\", self._username)))\n\n    def revoke_session(self, *, refresh_token: str) -> None:\n        #
